## Optional experimental tools

### `collaboration.preflight_receiver_handoff` · 接收端交接预检器 / Receiver Handoff Preflight

- State: `EXPERIMENTAL_AVAILABLE` / `EXPERIMENTAL` · version `0.1.0` · license `BUSL-1.1` · maintainer `Ignition repository maintainers; this field does not grant Owner or scientific authority.` · source blob `9dd3c2c9eaac9cfe955c760d5aa2107dbed5e64d`.
- Can do: Check an incoming serialized Federation handoff against its public source receipt, then evaluate receiver capability and workspace-reobservation preconditions without accepting or executing the handoff.
- Good for: Checking that an incoming typed handoff preserves the task, executor, validated completed work, artifacts, external-session pointers, and unresolved items bound by its canonical public result receipt.; Running the existing receiver capability and re-observation preconditions only after source-receipt/bundle projection equality passes.; Returning a machine-readable, read-only preflight before a human or separately authorized runtime decides whether to continue.
- Not for: Authenticating the person, executor, or system that created a receipt; the receipt digest is unkeyed.; Independently observing a workspace, verifying live receiver capabilities, authorizing a takeover, or dispatching/resuming an executor.; Proving that prior actions happened, artifacts are true, work is correct, or any scientific claim is valid.
- Use only if: The task has a serialized public FederatedResultReceipt and FederatedHandoffBundle using the existing strict contract shapes.; The caller treats workspace_reobserved and target_capabilities as caller declarations and preserves execution_authorized=false.; The input is a single UTF-8 JSON object no larger than 262144 bytes on stdin.
- Do not use when: The source receipt authenticity must be cryptographically authenticated or the sender identity is unknown and cannot be separately checked.; A live handoff, workspace read, network call, permission grant, provider action, or automatic takeover is expected.; A current runtime receiver protocol/version negotiation is required; this tool only uses the existing local Federation contract.
- Inputs → outputs: UTF-8 JSON object with schema_version receiver-handoff-preflight-r0, strict FederatedResultReceipt and FederatedHandoffBundle objects, target executor ID/capabilities, caller workspace_reobserved boolean, and observed artifact refs; maximum 262144 bytes. → Bounded JSON status for receipt digest, receipt-to-bundle projection, capability/re-observation preconditions, and typed mismatch findings., Explicit receipt_authenticity=NOT_ESTABLISHED_UNKEYED_DIGEST, execution_authorized=false, and runtime_enforcement_status=NOT_EVALUATED.
- Permissions: `READ_ONLY_RUN` / `READ_ONLY_SINGLE_HANDOFF_AND_LOCAL_FEDERATION_CONTRACTS` · explicit Owner authorization required `false` · reads `ignition/agent_federation/approval_handoff.py, ignition/agent_federation/contracts.py` · writes `none` · network `false` hosts `none` · secrets `false` · billing `false` · external actions `false.
- Side effects: Reads one bounded stdin object and local Federation code; writes one JSON result to stdout only..
- Dependencies: ignition/agent_federation/approval_handoff.py, ignition/agent_federation/contracts.py; runtime dependencies: Python >=3.9, Local Ignition agent_federation package; standard library only..
- Cost: `UNKNOWN` · No measured CPU, memory, stable latency, or matched cost profile has been collected.
- Alternatives: `collaboration.validate_agent_episode_binding` (COMPLEMENTARY_NOT_EQUIVALENT): That tool checks caller-declared multi-agent role, context, channel, permission, write-domain, and work-unit relationships. This tool checks serialized Federation receipt-to-handoff projection and receiver preconditions..
- Replacement candidates: none established.
- Unique remaining use cases: Before receiver takeover, reject a validly shaped handoff whose source-bound artifact, task/executor identity, validated work, session pointer, or unresolved items no longer match its public result receipt..
- Comparison evidence: `COMPARISON_UNKNOWN` · A matched synthetic negative control showed the existing accept_handoff function returns ACCEPTED for an altered artifact reference when the caller supplies source_receipt_verified=true and the altered reference as observed; this tool rejects the same receipt/bundle mismatch before receiver preconditions. This does not establish real-task quality or cost. Comparators: none registered; implementation refs: none recorded; refs: ignition/data/operations/toolbox-receiver-handoff-preflight-c1-evidence-r0.json, ignition/agent_federation/approval_handoff.py, ignition/tests/test_approval_handoff.py, ignition/tests/test_toolbox_receiver_handoff_preflight.py; findings: The candidate adds a receipt-to-bundle projection check that the current accept_handoff API does not perform.; The difference is supported only by synthetic contract fixtures; source authenticity, live receiver behavior, real task benefit, broad quality, and cost remain unknown..
- Deprecation/retirement: `ACTIVE` · Optional default-off specialization with synthetic-only evidence; no existing Federation or Agent Episode Binding component is replaced.
- Migration/rollback: No existing API or scheduler contract is migrated. Use only as a receiver preflight before a separate authorization and runtime decision. / Remove this optional candidate card, script, test, documentation, fixture, and C1 evidence; regenerate toolbox and identity-card projections; preserve prior commits and negative-control evidence..
- Invocation example: `python3 -B ignition/tools/toolbox_receiver_handoff_preflight.py --stdin`
- Fallback: If receipt binding does not pass, stop and require reconciliation. For an authentic or live takeover decision, use the existing Federation process only with independent receipt provenance, workspace re-observation, and current policy checks; never treat this preflight as authorization.
- Evidence: `SYNTHETIC_ONLY` · source refs `ignition/agent_federation/approval_handoff.py` (EXISTING_RECEIVER_TAKEOVER_BASELINE), `ignition/agent_federation/contracts.py` (CANONICAL_RECEIPT_AND_HANDOFF_TYPES), `ignition/data/operations/toolbox-receiver-handoff-preflight-c1-evidence-r0.json` (SYNTHETIC_MATCHED_CONFLICT_AND_C1_DISPOSITION), `ignition/docs/operations/toolbox-receiver-handoff-preflight.md` (USER_SCOPE_AND_LIMITS), `ignition/tests/fixtures/toolbox-receiver-handoff-preflight/valid-input-r0.json` (BOUNDED_CONTROLLED_CLI_EXAMPLE), `ignition/tests/test_approval_handoff.py` (EXISTING_API_CONTRACT_TESTS), `ignition/tests/test_toolbox_receiver_handoff_preflight.py` (FOCUSED_MATCHED_NEGATIVE_AND_CLI_TESTS), `ignition/tools/toolbox_receiver_handoff_preflight.py` (READ_ONLY_RECEIVER_PREFLIGHT_ENTRYPOINT); focused checks [ignition/tests/test_toolbox_receiver_handoff_preflight.py](tests/test_toolbox_receiver_handoff_preflight.py), [ignition/tests/test_toolbox_routing.py](tests/test_toolbox_routing.py), [ignition/tests/test_approval_handoff.py](tests/test_approval_handoff.py); negative controls: A changed source-bound artifact reference is rejected before receiver checks even when the caller reports it as observed.; Invalid source receipt digest, wrong target capability, ineligible handoff, absent workspace re-observation, duplicate JSON keys, and oversized stdin fail closed.; The valid checked-in example returns PREFLIGHT_READY_NO_AUTHORITY; execution_authorized remains false..

- Can do: Validate one caller-supplied portable research episode manifest for declared artifact-version, dependency, run/execution, review-target, omission, and read-only fork-ID relationships without reading or importing package contents.
- Good for: Checking that caller-declared artifact versions, parents and dependencies resolve consistently within one portable episode manifest.; Keeping PLANNED, ATTEMPTED, EXECUTED, NOT_EXECUTED and UNKNOWN declarations distinct, including separate used, attached and produced artifact references.; Pinning reviews to exact artifact versions, surfacing reviews of non-current versions, recording explicit omissions, and requiring unique identifier remaps for a read-only fork preview.
- Not for: Authenticating package bytes, source provenance, caller or actor identity, run records, review authors, permission declarations or digest truth.; Importing or copying packages, resolving forks, dispatching work, executing code, enforcing runtime permissions or writing files.; Establishing research reproducibility, scientific correctness, evidence sufficiency, publication readiness or Owner approval.
- Use only if: The caller supplies one UTF-8 JSON manifest conforming to the local R0 schema within the 262144-byte limit.; The result is treated only as internal consistency of caller declarations; any referenced run, digest, review and permission remains unauthenticated.; A portable episode manifest preflight is needed rather than a multi-agent plan check or a byte-only receipt check.
- Do not use when: The task requires independent authentication or byte verification of referenced files, runs, reviews, actors or sources.; The task requires importing, copying, executing, dispatching, changing permissions or making a publication or scientific decision.; Required package artifacts are unavailable and the task cannot tolerate caller-declared omissions or an incomplete status.
- Inputs → outputs: One UTF-8 JSON object conforming to ignition-portable-research-episode-r0 on stdin; maximum 262144 bytes. → Bounded deterministic JSON with schema and cross-reference status, typed findings and warnings, artifact/execution/review/omission/fork-map counts, and explicit non-authorization/non-import/non-byte-verification flags., Per-execution declared status and counts of used, attached and produced artifact references, without treating those caller declarations as authenticated events.
- Permissions: `READ_ONLY_RUN` / `READ_ONLY_SINGLE_CALLER_MANIFEST_AND_LOCAL_SCHEMA` · explicit Owner authorization required `false` · reads `ignition/schemas/operations/toolbox-portable-research-episode-r0.schema.json` · writes `none` · network `false` hosts `none` · secrets `false` · billing `false` · external actions `false.
- Side effects: Reads one bounded stdin manifest and the local schema; emits one bounded JSON result or validation error; does not read package paths or create or modify files..
- Dependencies: ignition/schemas/operations/toolbox-portable-research-episode-r0.schema.json; runtime dependencies: Python >=3.9, jsonschema >=4.18 with Draft 2020-12 support.
- Cost: `UNKNOWN` · No matched latency, CPU, memory, analyst-time, network or cash-cost comparison has been measured.
- Alternatives: `collaboration.validate_agent_episode_binding` (COMPLEMENTARY_NOT_EQUIVALENT): That tool validates a declared agent execution plan (roles, contexts, feedback channels, write domains and work-unit DAG); it does not validate portable artifact versions, run status relations, review-version targets, explicit omissions or fork ID maps.; `collaboration.verify_handoff_artifact_files` (COMPLEMENTARY_NOT_EQUIVALENT): That tool hashes bounded local files against a caller-supplied handoff receipt; it does not validate episode semantics, execution histories, review targets or fork mappings..
- Replacement candidates: none established.
- Unique remaining use cases: Validate a portable episode manifest that records artifact revisions and dependency links, run-state distinctions, separate used/attached/produced objects, version-pinned reviews, explicit omissions and complete ID remapping for a read-only fork preview..
- Comparison evidence: `COMPARISON_UNKNOWN` · Low-cost contract and route inspection compared the frozen portable-manifest task with collaboration.validate_agent_episode_binding and collaboration.verify_handoff_artifact_files. Their required input schemas and outputs cover adjacent but different objects. No fair same-task baseline, matched completion/quality trial or resource-cost comparison was available, so relative correctness, reliability and cost remain unknown. The tools may be complementary in a larger handoff, but are not equivalent.; The PR #264 fixture invocation checks a real task-derived, caller-declared manifest and returns internally consistent relations; it does not authenticate the underlying Actions run, fixture digests or execution claim.; C1 disposition remains COMPARISON_UNKNOWN. - Migration/rollback: No Current registry operation or existing tool is migrated. ### `collaboration.verify_handoff_artifact_files` · 交接产物字节核验器 / Handoff Artifact Byte Verifier

- State: `EXPERIMENTAL_AVAILABLE` / `EXPERIMENTAL` · version `0.1.0` · license `BUSL-1.1` · maintainer `Ignition repository maintainers; this field does not grant Owner or scientific authority.` · source blob `bd8b7062be93e311734143df4611e943eccee1f8`.
- Can do: Compare bounded bytes from local files under an explicit ignition/ workspace root with the SHA-256 values in a typed FederatedResultReceipt, without returning artifact contents or authorizing a handoff.
- Good for: Checking whether the current local bytes of receipt-referenced artifacts under a caller-selected ignition/ workspace root match the receipt's declared SHA-256 values.; Hashing bounded regular files, including non-UTF-8 and binary artifacts, without returning their contents.; Adding a local byte observation before a separate receiver or reviewer decision when an existing handoff preflight only checks declarations and receipt-to-bundle binding.
- Not for: Authenticating the receipt, its author, executor, or source; its self-digest is unkeyed.; Proving artifact provenance, stable state after the read, task completion, semantic correctness, scientific validity, or receiver authorization.; Reading paths outside ignition/, contacting a provider, dispatching or accepting a handoff, writing files, or enforcing runtime isolation.
- Use only if: The caller supplies one valid FederatedResultReceipt with 1 to 64 artifact references and an explicit workspace root under ignition/.; Every artifact reference is a safe relative POSIX path under that selected root, and each file is expected to be a regular file no larger than 8 MiB within the 32 MiB total byte budget.; The caller accepts an unkeyed digest comparison and preserves execution_authorized=false as well as runtime_enforcement_status=NOT_EVALUATED.
- Do not use when: Receipt provenance or sender identity must be authenticated, or the task needs a trusted source or stable snapshot guarantee.; The artifacts are outside ignition/, exceed the declared count/byte limits, or require following symlinks or reading non-regular files.; The requested outcome requires semantic review, task correctness, a handoff decision, permission grant, network action, or scientific acceptance.
- Inputs → outputs: One UTF-8 JSON object with schema_version handoff-artifact-byte-check-r0 and a valid FederatedResultReceipt containing artifact refs, plus a separately supplied workspace-root CLI argument under ignition/; stdin is capped at 262144 bytes. → Per-artifact expected and observed SHA-256 statuses, byte counts, and typed read/path/size failures for bounded regular-file bytes; no file contents are returned., Explicit receipt_authenticity=NOT_ESTABLISHED_UNKEYED_DIGEST, execution_authorized=false, and runtime_enforcement_status=NOT_EVALUATED.
- Permissions: `READ_ONLY_RUN` / `READ_ONLY_BOUNDED_LOCAL_ARTIFACT_HASHING` · explicit Owner authorization required `false` · reads `ignition/` · writes `none` · network `false` hosts `none` · secrets `false` · billing `false` · external actions `false.
- Side effects: Reads only caller-referenced regular-file bytes under the explicit workspace root with bounded descriptor-based opens; prints one bounded JSON result to stdout..
- Dependencies: ignition/agent_federation/contracts.py; runtime dependencies: Python >=3.9, Local Ignition agent_federation.contracts types; otherwise standard library only., POSIX O_NOFOLLOW, O_DIRECTORY, and dir_fd support; unsupported platforms fail closed..
- Cost: `UNKNOWN` · No measured CPU, memory, latency, or matched alternative cost profile has been collected; per-invocation file reads are capped at 32 MiB.
- Alternatives: `collaboration.preflight_receiver_handoff` (COMPLEMENTARY_NOT_EQUIVALENT): The receiver preflight checks receipt-to-bundle projection and caller-declared receiver preconditions without reading referenced file bytes; this candidate checks current bounded local bytes without deciding takeover readiness.; `knowledge.assemble_context_packet` (SPECIALIZED_NOT_EQUIVALENT): The context packet also checks current file digests but requires UTF-8 text context, line ranges, and obligation mapping and emits excerpts; this candidate is receipt-native, hashes binary artifacts, and emits no file contents..
- Replacement candidates: none established.
- Unique remaining use cases: Verify receipt-bound bytes of binary or non-UTF-8 artifacts under a caller-selected ignition/ workspace without returning artifact contents.; Add current local-byte digest comparison to a Federation handoff review while leaving authenticity, provenance, and takeover decisions to separate authority..
- Comparison evidence: `SPECIALIZED_NO_REPLACEMENT_ESTABLISHED` · Matched synthetic controls show that the receiver preflight accepts caller-declared artifact refs without checking changed local bytes, while the context packet hashes UTF-8 context files but classifies the same binary artifact as SOURCE_NOT_UTF8. The candidate returns DIGEST_MISMATCH for the first case and DIGEST_MATCH for the binary case without emitting file contents. No real-task quality, broad reliability, cost, authenticity, provenance, or stable-snapshot conclusion is established. Comparators: collaboration.preflight_receiver_handoff, knowledge.assemble_context_packet; implementation refs: none recorded; refs: ignition/data/operations/toolbox-handoff-artifact-byte-check-c1-evidence-r0.json, ignition/tests/test_toolbox_context_packet.py, ignition/tests/test_toolbox_handoff_artifact_file_verifier.py, ignition/tests/test_approval_handoff.py; findings: The receiver preflight and byte verifier serve consecutive, distinct handoff stages: declaration consistency first, current local byte comparison only when separately requested.; The context packet has a distinct text-context and obligation-mapping output; its UTF-8 excerpt behavior does not cover binary artifacts or digest-only handling without content output.; Retain both existing tools and add this optional specialization; evidence does not support replacing or retiring any current or candidate tool, and cost remains unknown..
- Deprecation/retirement: `SPECIALIZED` · A synthetic matched comparison supports a separate binary-capable, no-content artifact-byte check; it does not establish that the receiver preflight or context packet should be retired or replaced.
- Migration/rollback: No Current registry operation or existing candidate is migrated. Keep the receiver preflight for receipt-to-bundle and receiver-declaration checks; add this tool only when actual local artifact-byte comparison is required.; Use the context packet when a task needs UTF-8 excerpts and structural obligation linkage; do not route those tasks here merely to obtain a digest. / Remove this candidate card, entrypoint, docs, fixtures, tests, comparison/route evidence, and regenerate toolbox identity projections on the isolated branch; preserve prior evidence commits for history..
- Invocation example: `python3 -B ignition/tools/toolbox_verify_handoff_artifact_files.py --stdin --workspace-root <path-under-ignition>`
- Fallback: If any ref, root, size, or digest check fails, stop before receiver takeover and reconcile the receipt, workspace, and artifact bytes. Use a separate provenance/authenticity process when trust is required; never infer authorization from a digest match.
- Evidence: `SYNTHETIC_ONLY` · source refs `ignition/agent_federation/contracts.py` (CANONICAL_FEDERATED_RECEIPT_AND_ARTIFACT_REF_TYPES), `ignition/data/operations/toolbox-handoff-artifact-byte-check-c1-evidence-r0.json` (SYNTHETIC_MATCHED_COMPARISON_AND_C1_DISPOSITION), `ignition/data/operations/toolbox-handoff-artifact-byte-check-route-profile-r0.json` (EXPLICIT_LOCAL_ROUTE_PROFILE), `ignition/data/operations/toolbox-handoff-artifact-byte-check-route-evidence-r0.json` (DEFAULT_OFF_AND_ALLOWLISTED_ROUTE_RESULTS), `ignition/docs/operations/toolbox-handoff-artifact-byte-check.md` (USER_SCOPE_COMPARATORS_AND_LIMITS), `ignition/tests/fixtures/toolbox-handoff-artifact-byte-check/valid-input-r0.json` (BOUNDED_CONTROLLED_CLI_EXAMPLE), `ignition/tests/fixtures/toolbox-handoff-artifact-byte-check/workspace/` (LOCAL_NONAUTHORITATIVE_FIXTURE_ARTIFACT_TREE), `ignition/tests/test_approval_handoff.py` (EXISTING_RECEIVER_API_CONTRACT_TESTS), `ignition/tests/test_toolbox_context_packet.py` (EXISTING_UTF8_CONTEXT_PACKET_BASELINE_TESTS), `ignition/tests/test_toolbox_handoff_artifact_file_verifier.py` (FOCUSED_BYTE_BOUNDARY_AND_MATCHED_NEGATIVE_TESTS), `ignition/tests/test_toolbox_identity_cards.py` (IDENTITY_CARD_AND_C1_ASSERTIONS), `ignition/tests/test_toolbox_routing.py` (DEFAULT_OFF_AND_EXPLICIT_ROUTE_TESTS), `ignition/tools/toolbox_context_packet.py` (TEXT_EXCERPT_DIGEST_COMPARATOR), `ignition/tools/toolbox_receiver_handoff_preflight.py` (RECEIPT_PROJECTION_PRECHECK_COMPARATOR), `ignition/tools/toolbox_verify_handoff_artifact_files.py` (READ_ONLY_BOUNDED_ARTIFACT_BYTE_ENTRYPOINT); negative controls: With the same receipt ref and a changed local file, preflight_receiver_handoff returns PREFLIGHT_READY_NO_AUTHORITY while this checker returns DIGEST_MISMATCH.; A binary non-UTF-8 artifact returns DIGEST_MATCH without its contents; the context packet returns SOURCE_NOT_UTF8 and no excerpt for the same file.; Traversal, URI refs, symlinks, outside roots, non-regular files, byte/count limits, duplicate JSON keys, invalid receipts, and oversized stdin fail closed.; The router excludes the candidate by default and proposes it only under its explicit experimental allowlist; route output never authorizes execution..
- Route default: `false` · conditions: Only select through an explicit experimental-tool allowlist when a task requires current bytes for receipt-referenced local artifacts under ignition/ and needs no artifact content output.; Do not select for UTF-8 excerpt or obligation-mapping work covered by knowledge.assemble_context_packet, or for receipt-to-bundle and receiver-declaration checks covered by collaboration.preflight_receiver_handoff.; A route is a proposal only; the caller separately decides whether to invoke the read-only tool, and digest match never authorizes a handoff..
- Claim ceiling: Checks whether bounded bytes read from regular files under an explicit repository-local ignition/ workspace root match the SHA-256 values in a typed FederatedResultReceipt whose canonical unkeyed self-digest passes. It does not authenticate the receipt or source, prove file provenance or task correctness, observe a stable runtime workspace after reading, authorize actions, enforce runtime isolation, or establish scientific truth.

### `foundation.read_current_asset_card` · Current Foundation 身份卡读取器 / Current Foundation record reader

- State: `EXPERIMENTAL_AVAILABLE` / `EXPERIMENTAL` · version `0.1.0` · license `BUSL-1.1` · maintainer `Ignition repository maintainers; this field does not grant Owner or scientific authority.` · source blob `80b1d6ebd20ec7107ff158d8f75d76f8cfb7dbd6`.
- Can do: Read one exact canonical ID from the checked-out Current function-identity or non-function-claim registry; return the complete stored record and verify its record SHA-256.
- Good for: Read the complete current record after the caller already has an exact canonical ID.; Inspect the record kind, authority fields, evidence references, use boundaries, and verified record digest in one deterministic JSON result.
- Not for: Discovering IDs, resolving aliases or historical references, fuzzy search, or selecting between ambiguous records.; Checking that linked source files still support the record, validating scientific claims, or establishing proof or external validity.; Changing registries or writing a dossier into the repository.
- Use only if: The caller supplies one exact canonical ID already listed in a Current registry.; The function identity and non-function claim registries are available in the same checked-out snapshot.; The caller accepts a full record payload on stdout and record-level SHA-256 verification only.
- Do not use when: The input is a title, alias, historical path, natural-language query, or uncertain identifier.; The task needs fresh external evidence, independent source-anchor checking, scientific adjudication, or an authority upgrade.; The caller needs only a compact yes/no identity resolution; use the existing resolver instead.
- Inputs → outputs: JSON stdin object containing exactly one exact canonical_id string → complete Current function-identity card or non-function claim record, verified record digest and hashes of both registry snapshots
- Permissions: `READ_ONLY_RUN` / `READ_ONLY_REPOSITORY_SNAPSHOT` · explicit Owner authorization required `false` · reads `ignition/data/foundation/function-assets/identity-cards.jsonl, ignition/data/foundation/nonfunction-claims/claim-registry.jsonl` · writes `none` · network `false` hosts `none` · secrets `false` · billing `false` · external actions `false.
- Side effects: Writes the record result only to stdout; invalid input or unavailable/corrupt authorities return a nonzero status and no partial stdout..
- Dependencies: ignition/data/foundation/function-assets/identity-cards.jsonl, ignition/data/foundation/nonfunction-claims/claim-registry.jsonl; runtime dependencies: Python >=3.9 standard library.
- Cost: `UNKNOWN` · Both checked-out JSONL registries are scanned per call; latency and memory have not been benchmarked.
- Alternatives: `foundation.batch_resolve_current_asset_references` (COMPLEMENTARY_NOT_EQUIVALENT): The batch resolver accepts aliases and historical references but returns bounded resolution rows; this reader accepts one canonical ID and returns its full current registry record..
- Replacement candidates: none established.
- Unique remaining use cases: not established.
- Comparison evidence: `COMPARISON_UNKNOWN` · Existing evidence tests this candidate in isolation; no paired same-task comparison against its mapped alternatives has been recorded for C1. Comparators: none registered; implementation refs: none recorded; refs: ignition/tools/toolbox_read_current_asset_card.py, ignition/tests/test_toolbox_read_current_asset_card.py, ignition/tests/test_current_canonical_resolution.py; findings: No replacement, unique-use, or retirement conclusion is established; preserve the current optional/default-off disposition pending a task-paired comparison where overlap matters..
- Deprecation/retirement: `ACTIVE` · The optional experimental entry remains available and default-off; comparative retirement status is unknown.
- Migration/rollback: No automatic migration is inferred; preserve this exact input/output contract until a same-task comparison supports specialization or replacement. / Remove the candidate entry and wrapper from the isolated toolbox branch, regenerate the identity and human-directory projections, and preserve prior commits and evidence..

- Fallback: Use foundation.batch_resolve_current_asset_references to confirm exact Current identity resolution; for full record content, read the authority row through a separately approved repository reader and verify its record_sha256.
- Evidence: `REAL_TASK_OUTCOME` · source refs `ignition/tools/toolbox_read_current_asset_card.py` (READ_ONLY_TOOL_ENTRYPOINT), `ignition/data/foundation/function-assets/identity-cards.jsonl` (CURRENT_FUNCTION_IDENTITY_AUTHORITY), `ignition/data/foundation/nonfunction-claims/claim-registry.jsonl` (CURRENT_NONFUNCTION_CLAIM_AUTHORITY), `ignition/tests/test_toolbox_read_current_asset_card.py` (C1_FUNCTIONAL_EVIDENCE_REFERENCE), `ignition/tests/test_current_canonical_resolution.py` (C1_FUNCTIONAL_EVIDENCE_REFERENCE); focused checks [ignition/tests/test_toolbox_read_current_asset_card.py](tests/test_toolbox_read_current_asset_card.py), [ignition/tests/test_current_canonical_resolution.py](tests/test_current_canonical_resolution.py); negative controls: Aliases, titles, paths, malformed IDs, and unknown IDs are rejected without partial stdout.; A duplicate canonical ID across function and claim registries, a corrupt record hash, or a symlinked authority fails closed.; The result marks only the stored record hash as verified and states that linked-source support and scientific validity are not established..

- Claim ceiling: One exact checked-out Current registry record, with its stored record hash verified and both identity registries source-hashed; no truth, proof, external validity, or independent source-anchor verification is inferred.

### `function_os.run_bounded_symbolic_spec` · 有界符号函数规格执行器 / Bounded symbolic FunctionSpec runner

- State: `EXPERIMENTAL_AVAILABLE` / `EXPERIMENTAL` · version `toolbox-wrapper-0.1.0; Function OS 0.2.1rc0 candidate` · license `BUSL-1.1` · maintainer `Ignition repository maintainers; this field does not grant Owner or scientific authority.` · source blob `bcb1537700d14acff651aabb5ddbe7f311561c27`.
- Can do: Run one explicitly supplied, single-output pure symbolic FunctionSpec through Function OS N1-N7 with bounded expression size, arithmetic intermediates, and numeric input magnitude.
- Good for: Run one declared deterministic symbolic function with one output and 1 to 16 explicitly supplied numeric or boolean inputs.; Inspect the N1-N7 execution status, in-memory artifact integrity report, trace, and N7 internal consistency checks for a bounded specification.
- Not for: Executing untrusted or malicious code, acting as an isolated security sandbox, or processing arbitrary Python expressions.; Scientific adjudication, proof, empirical validation, external truth claims, or recognition that a function models reality.; Stateful or I/O effects, multiple outputs, exponentiation, calls, attributes, N8 routing, N9 registry writes, or persisted artifacts.
- Use only if: Python 3.10 or newer is available and the checked-out Function OS v0.2 source and wrapper are intact.; The caller supplies a single symbolic FunctionSpec with exactly one output, exactly pure effects, 1 to 16 declared inputs, and one output equality postcondition.; The UTF-8 JSON request is at most 16 KiB; expressions are at most 512 characters, 64 AST nodes, and depth 16; numeric inputs/constants are at most 10^12 in magnitude and arithmetic intermediates/results at most 10^100.
- Do not use when: Inputs or expressions are adversarial and require operating-system isolation or a security boundary.; The specification needs exponentiation, calls, attributes, strings, collections, multiple outputs, non-pure effects, or values beyond the declared bounds.; The task requires persistence, registry admission, source-backed evidence, scientific truth, proof, or external validation.
- Inputs → outputs: UTF-8 JSON stdin object containing exactly one bounded FunctionSpec object and one exact input-value object → N1-N7 execution status and single declared output when successful, in-memory artifact integrity report, execution trace, and N7 internal consistency result, source-tree and wrapper source digests
- Permissions: `READ_ONLY_RUN` / `READ_ONLY_REPOSITORY_SOURCE_AND_STDIN` · explicit Owner authorization required `false` · reads `ignition/tools/toolbox_function_os_evaluator.py, ignition/function-os-candidate/v0.2/function_os/, ignition/function-os-candidate/v0.2/v0.2-scope-contract.json, ignition/function-os-candidate/v0.2/pyproject.toml` · writes `none` · network `false` hosts `none` · secrets `false` · billing `false` · external actions `false.
- Side effects: Writes the bounded JSON result to stdout; execution artifacts and traces remain in process memory and N9 is not invoked., The wrapper disables Python bytecode writes before loading Function OS modules..
- Dependencies: ignition/function-os-candidate/v0.2/function_os/, ignition/function-os-candidate/v0.2/v0.2-scope-contract.json, ignition/function-os-candidate/v0.2/pyproject.toml; runtime dependencies: Python >=3.10 standard library; no third-party runtime packages.
- Cost: `UNKNOWN` · Execution is local and bounded by request and AST limits, but no latency or memory benchmark has been established.
- Alternatives: none mapped.
- Replacement candidates: none established.
- Unique remaining use cases: not established.
- Comparison evidence: `COMPARISON_UNKNOWN` · Existing evidence tests this candidate in isolation; no paired same-task comparison against its mapped alternatives has been recorded for C1. Comparators: none registered; implementation refs: none recorded; refs: ignition/tools/toolbox_function_os_evaluator.py, ignition/tests/test_toolbox_function_os_evaluator.py; findings: No replacement, unique-use, or retirement conclusion is established; preserve the current optional/default-off disposition pending a task-paired comparison where overlap matters..
- Deprecation/retirement: `ACTIVE` · The optional experimental entry remains available and default-off; comparative retirement status is unknown.
- Migration/rollback: No automatic migration is inferred; preserve this exact input/output contract until a same-task comparison supports specialization or replacement. / Remove the candidate entry and wrapper from the isolated toolbox branch, regenerate the identity and human-directory projections, and preserve prior commits and evidence..
- Invocation example: `python3.11 -B ignition/tools/toolbox_function_os_evaluator.py --stdin (stdin JSON: {"function_spec":{...},"inputs":{...}})`
- Fallback: Use the checked-in Function OS N1-N7 tests on Python 3.10 or newer and inspect the exact specification and expected outputs manually; do not substitute eval, a general Python runner, or a scientific-validity claim.
- Evidence: `REAL_TOOL_TEST` · source refs `ignition/tools/toolbox_function_os_evaluator.py` (BOUNDED_READ_ONLY_TOOL_ENTRYPOINT), `ignition/function-os-candidate/v0.2/function_os/` (FUNCTION_OS_N1_TO_N7_IMPLEMENTATION), `ignition/function-os-candidate/v0.2/v0.2-scope-contract.json` (UPSTREAM_SCOPE_AND_NON_GOAL_CONTRACT), `ignition/function-os-candidate/v0.2/pyproject.toml` (UPSTREAM_VERSION_LICENSE_AND_RUNTIME_METADATA), `ignition/tests/test_toolbox_function_os_evaluator.py` (C1_FUNCTIONAL_EVIDENCE_REFERENCE); focused checks [ignition/tests/test_toolbox_function_os_evaluator.py](tests/test_toolbox_function_os_evaluator.py); negative controls: Calls, attributes, exponentiation, multiple outputs, non-pure effects, oversized inputs, and oversized intermediate arithmetic are rejected.; Duplicate JSON keys and requests over 16 KiB return nonzero with no partial stdout; division by zero is rejected by the bounded arithmetic preflight.; Precondition failure is preserved; N9 registry writes are never invoked; a passing N7 status is explicitly limited to internal consistency..
- Route default: `false` · conditions: Include only through an explicit experimental-tool allowlist when the task supplies a bounded pure symbolic FunctionSpec and asks for its local N1-N7 execution report.; A route proposal never runs the function; the caller separately supplies the complete FunctionSpec and inputs to the CLI.; Never select for arbitrary code, scientific proof, truth, registry admission, or effectful execution..
- Claim ceiling: A bounded local execution report for one pure symbolic FunctionSpec through Function OS N1-N7, with internal artifact/trace consistency checks only; it does not establish specification truth, empirical or scientific correctness, proof, external validation, security isolation, registry admission, or publication status.

### `ignition.adaptive_toolbox_route_planner` · 点火工具组合路由预览 / Ignition toolbox route preview

- State: `EXPERIMENTAL_AVAILABLE` / `EXPERIMENTAL` · version `0.1.0` · license `BUSL-1.1` · maintainer `Ignition repository maintainers; this field does not grant Owner or scientific authority.` · source blob `d7881daae28a3d82612ceadb78d77bcd879bfe83`.
- Can do: Select a sufficient set of registered operations for exact input and output requirements, optionally considering explicitly allowlisted experimental cards and exact caller-supplied network hosts.
- Good for: Planning routes from explicit input types and required output types.; Showing when a task needs one operation, complementary operations, or remains uncovered.; Keeping experimental tools opt-in and returning an explicit incomplete result when no declared route covers a requirement.; Keeping fixed-host network candidates excluded unless the typed profile explicitly lists every exact required DNS host.
- Not for: Inferring the task contract from unstructured natural language.; Calling, dispatching, or executing selected operations.; Granting permissions, Owner approval, or scientific authority.; Claiming measured cost optimality or a globally minimal set.; Authenticating the caller authority behind an allowed_network_hosts value or authorizing a network request.
- Use only if: The caller supplies exact input types, required outputs, allowed modes, and a nonblank Current reference string; the planner echoes that string without resolving it.; The operation registry and pinned experimental candidate-card source are present.; Optional allowed_network_hosts entries are caller-supplied route context only and never grant authority; actual invocation and permissions are checked separately.
- Do not use when: Task requirements cannot be expressed with the declared input/output types.; The caller needs actual tool execution or a scientific conclusion rather than a route proposal.; The current operation registry no longer matches the candidate-card source pin.
- Inputs → outputs: typed toolbox route profile on stdin, optional allowed_network_hosts exact DNS-name list; omitted means no network hosts allowed → typed route proposal, covered and unmet output list, candidate rejection reasons, caller-supplied network-host context and its unverified binding status

- Side effects: stdout only.
- Dependencies: ignition/data/operations/ignition-operation-capability-registry-r1.json, ignition/data/operations/toolbox-candidate-cards-r0.json, ignition/tools/operations/classify_ignition_run_mode.py; runtime dependencies: Python >=3.9 standard library.
- Cost: `UNKNOWN` · The selection run is local, but no calibrated latency, memory, or compute-cost comparison exists.
- Alternatives: `repository.apply_iteration_method` (NOT_EQUIVALENT): The existing single-operation lifecycle planner resolves one declared operation; this preview selects a typed set and still does not execute it..
- Replacement candidates: none established.
- Unique remaining use cases: not established.
- Comparison evidence: `COMPARISON_UNKNOWN` · Existing evidence tests this candidate in isolation; no paired same-task comparison against its mapped alternatives has been recorded for C1. Comparators: none registered; implementation refs: none recorded; refs: ignition/tools/operations/plan_ignition_operation_run.py, ignition/tests/test_ignition_operation_lifecycle.py, ignition/tests/test_toolbox_routing.py; findings: No replacement, unique-use, or retirement conclusion is established; preserve the current optional/default-off disposition pending a task-paired comparison where overlap matters..
- Deprecation/retirement: `ACTIVE` · The optional experimental entry remains available and default-off; comparative retirement status is unknown.
- Migration/rollback: No automatic migration is inferred; preserve this exact input/output contract until a same-task comparison supports specialization or replacement. / Remove the candidate entry and wrapper from the isolated toolbox branch, regenerate the identity and human-directory projections, and preserve prior commits and evidence..

### `ignition.select_bounded_thinking_operation` · 有界思考操作选择器 / Bounded Thinking Operation Selector

- State: `EXPERIMENTAL_AVAILABLE` / `EXPERIMENTAL` · version `0.1.0` · license `BUSL-1.1` · maintainer `Ignition repository maintainers; this field grants no Owner, executor, or scientific authority.` · source blob `04d0b7e8036e77b8419650d845125d571f772029`.
- Can do: From caller-declared state of an already bounded run, recommend one next thinking-operation class or a terminal STOP; it neither chooses a Goal nor selects or executes concrete tools.
- Good for: Proposing one next thinking-operation class from explicit authority, frame, capability, freshness, verification, decomposition, obligation, failure-history and budget state inside an already bounded run.; Returning explicit STOP dispositions when a required operation is unavailable, the declared budget is insufficient, expected value is not positive, or a required obligation remains unresolved.; Re-evaluating after the caller records an observable operation result, including a new verification obligation revealed by a freshness read.
- Not for: Selecting or changing Intent, Goal, authority, the task frame, the toolbox route, or the executor.; Executing READ_MORE, VERIFY, escalation, owner contact, or any other operation; a proposal is not an invocation or permission grant.; Reading or exposing hidden chain-of-thought, establishing source truth, completing a Goal, or upgrading scientific evidence.; Replacing the adaptive toolbox route planner, which selects concrete tools for typed input/output requirements.
- Use only if: The caller has already admitted a bounded run and supplies a schema-valid, current caller-declared state with explicit references and required outputs.; Each non-STOP candidate has caller-declared positive expected value, abstract cost, required capabilities, and a return condition; cost units are planning inputs, not measured resource costs.; The caller separately authorizes and performs any suggested operation, records its observable outcome, and submits a new state for reconsideration.
- Do not use when: Goal/authority/frame state is unknown, stale, untrusted, or cannot be declared without inventing facts.; The request expects the selector to execute a tool, grant permission, contact an Owner, decide truth, or complete an obligation automatically.; The task requires calibrated cost estimates, live-model metacognition, or production-level effectiveness claims.
- Inputs → outputs: UTF-8 JSON stdin object matching toolbox-bounded-thinking-operation-input-r0; one bounded run with explicit task refs, state/obligations, available operation/capability lists, and candidate assessments; maximum 262144 bytes. → One thinking-operation proposal READ_MORE, VERIFY, DECOMPOSE, REFRAME_OR_CLARIFY, CAPACITY_OR_EXECUTOR_ESCALATE, REQUEST_OWNER_DECISION, or STOP with a bounded terminal disposition., Structured reasons and refs, expected state delta/value/cost declarations when present, preserved first failure, and fixed no-authority/no-execution flags.
- Permissions: `READ_ONLY_RUN` / `READ_ONLY_LOCAL_SCHEMA_READ` · explicit Owner authorization required `false` · reads `ignition/schemas/operations/toolbox-bounded-thinking-operation-input-r0.schema.json, ignition/schemas/operations/toolbox-bounded-thinking-operation-output-r0.schema.json` · writes `none` · network `false` hosts `none` · secrets `false` · billing `false` · external actions `false.
- Side effects: Reads stdin and the two local schemas; writes one JSON result to stdout only..
- Dependencies: ignition/schemas/operations/toolbox-bounded-thinking-operation-input-r0.schema.json, ignition/schemas/operations/toolbox-bounded-thinking-operation-output-r0.schema.json; runtime dependencies: Python >=3.9, jsonschema with Draft 2020-12 support.
- Cost: `UNKNOWN` · One local process timing is recorded but is not a calibrated latency, compute, memory, or cost benchmark; the input cost bands are caller-declared.
- Alternatives: `ignition.adaptive_toolbox_route_planner` (COMPLEMENTARY_NOT_EQUIVALENT): The existing planner selects a sufficient set of concrete tools for typed inputs and outputs; this selector chooses the next thinking-operation class inside an already bounded run. Neither performs the other’s job..
- Replacement candidates: none established.
- Unique remaining use cases: Select one next thinking-operation class from caller-declared within-run state without selecting a Goal or concrete tool set..
- Comparison evidence: `COMPARISON_UNKNOWN` · One bounded tool-admission task was observed, but the compared systems emit different output types and operate at different layers; broad quality, cost, reliability, and maintenance comparisons remain unknown. Comparators: ignition.adaptive_toolbox_route_planner; implementation refs: ignition/tools/operations/plan_ignition_operation_run.py; refs: ignition/data/operations/toolbox-bounded-thinking-operation-blueprint-reference-r0.json, ignition/data/operations/toolbox-bounded-thinking-operation-c1-evidence-r0.json, ignition/data/operations/toolbox-bounded-thinking-operation-real-task-r0.json, ignition/data/operations/toolbox-bounded-thinking-operation-route-evidence-r0.json; findings: Workbench AB-16 results are frozen synthetic mechanism evidence only; they do not establish the Formal CLI as a live-model or production system.; The new selector recommends an intra-run thinking operation; the adaptive planner proposes concrete tools for typed coverage, so they are complementary and non-equivalent.; No tool is retired, deprecated, or claimed as a replacement; measured comparative quality and cost are unknown..
- Deprecation/retirement: `COMPARISON_UNKNOWN` · This default-off experimental candidate has one bounded real-task observation and a synthetic-only upstream pilot; no same-output comparison supports a replacement or retirement decision.
- Migration/rollback: No caller migrates from the adaptive toolbox route planner; explicitly allowlist this candidate only for the within-run thinking-operation output contract. / Remove this experimental card and CLI from the isolated branch after checking consumers, regenerate toolbox-identity-cards-r0.json and TOOLBOX.md, rerun focused checks, and retain the blueprint/real-task evidence as history..

### `knowledge.assemble_context_packet` · 有界上下文包组装器 / Bounded Context Packet Assembler

- State: `EXPERIMENTAL_AVAILABLE` / `EXPERIMENTAL` · version `0.1.0` · license `BUSL-1.1` · maintainer `Ignition repository maintainers; this field does not grant Owner or scientific authority.` · source blob `1c6e6f5fdf4ceb2ef0f75d17dd0354c9b8daa301`.
- Can do: Assemble caller-selected repository excerpts into one digest-checked ContextPacket with structural obligation links and explicit gaps; never evaluate semantic support or sufficiency.
- Good for: Packaging multiple caller-selected local source excerpts under one task/question ID with exact file digests and line ranges.; Showing which declared context obligations have current byte-matching inputs and which remain missing, stale, generated-only, unreadable, partial, or over budget.; Preparing a structured context handoff where semantic support and sufficiency must remain an explicit later decision.
- Not for: Discovering sources, resolving a Current ref, authenticating source authority, or deciding semantic relevance.; Proving that a source supports an obligation, that the packet is complete, or that a conclusion is true.; Turning a generated summary into source evidence or authorizing retrieval, network access, publication, or repository writes.
- Use only if: The caller provides exact source paths, SHA-256 digests, line ranges, obligation IDs, and an explicit aggregate byte budget.; All requested files are under the authorized checked-out ignition/ tree and excerpt output is permitted for its intended audience.; The expected result is structural packet assembly only; a separate reviewer will assess source meaning and sufficiency.
- Do not use when: The task requires external or newly discoverable sources, authoritative Current-ref resolution, semantic coverage, or scientific judgment.; A source digest is unknown, a path is outside ignition/, or the requested range cannot be bounded.; Selected local text may be sensitive and the caller or output audience is not authorized to receive it; the tool does not redact excerpts.

### `knowledge.navigate_raw_evidence` · 原始证据导航器 / Raw evidence navigator

- State: `EXPERIMENTAL_AVAILABLE` / `EXPERIMENTAL` · version `0.1.0` · license `BUSL-1.1` · maintainer `Ignition repository maintainers; this field does not grant Owner or scientific authority.` · source blob `c8ef67442dcf90a9ef657e725b7562f63c392988`.
- Can do: Use the checked-out Knowledge search index as a navigation prior, reopen bounded current raw files, and return exact line matches with source hashes.
- Good for: Finding repository-local source candidates from explicit literal query terms when the caller does not know an asset ID or article-cluster ID.; Reopening indexed source files from the current checkout and returning line numbers, current SHA-256 values, and whether the indexed digest differs.; Keeping incomplete or budget-limited navigation visible as an unresolved gap.
- Not for: Semantic retrieval, query expansion, or discovery of newly added sources absent from the checked-out search index.; Deciding that any source supports a proposition, that a requirement is satisfied, or that independent source-family support exists.; Replacing a live web search, scientific review, source-authenticity check, proof, or publication decision.
- Use only if: The requested task is local repository navigation and supplies 1 to 12 literal query terms for each of 1 to 8 named requirements.; The checked-out Knowledge search index and current source files are the intended scope.; The caller accepts an explicit total raw-source byte budget, a per-requirement source limit, and a result ceiling of navigation hints only.
- Do not use when: The task requires current external facts or sources outside the checked-out Formal repository.; The relevant source may be new or unindexed and the caller needs a completeness guarantee.; A semantic, causal, mathematical, or scientific judgment is required rather than literal source navigation.; The selected repository excerpts may contain sensitive text; use only when the current caller and output audience are authorized to read those files because the tool does not redact source content.
- Inputs → outputs: Bounded UTF-8 JSON stdin with requirement IDs, literal query terms, maximum sources per requirement, total raw-source byte budget, and maximum excerpt length → Ranked Knowledge-index candidates with exact repository source paths and indexed/current SHA-256 comparison., Current raw line matches with line numbers, matched and missing query terms, current/indexed SHA-256 comparison, at most 8 excerpts per source, matched-line count, truncation flag, and explicit partial/unopened/missing statuses., Requirement-level navigation hints and unresolved gaps; sufficiency_decision is always NOT_EVALUATED.
- Permissions: `READ_ONLY_RUN` / `READ_ONLY_SEARCH_INDEX_AND_INDEX_SELECTED_SOURCES_UNDER_IGNITION` · explicit Owner authorization required `false` · reads `ignition/` · writes `none` · network `false` hosts `none` · secrets `false` · billing `false` · external actions `false.
- Side effects: Reads the fixed checked-out Knowledge index and bounded indexed source files; emits one JSON result to stdout and writes no repository or temporary files..
- Dependencies: ignition/data/governance/knowledge-experience/search-index.jsonl; runtime dependencies: Python >=3.9 standard library.
- Cost: `UNKNOWN` · The current fixed index is about 20.5 MB and 24695 rows; raw-source reads are caller-bounded to at most 2 MB, but latency and memory profiles are not calibrated.
- Alternatives: `knowledge.cluster_source_brief` (COMPLEMENTARY_NOT_EQUIVALENT): That tool renders a declared cluster by ID; this tool accepts explicit query terms and reopens indexed raw sources.; `foundation.read_current_asset_card` (COMPLEMENTARY_NOT_EQUIVALENT): That tool reads one exact canonical record ID; this tool navigates from literal terms without requiring an ID..
- Replacement candidates: none established.
- Unique remaining use cases: Offline query-conditioned navigation from the checked-out Knowledge index to current raw source lines when the caller does not know a canonical asset or cluster ID.; Detecting that a currently reopened source digest differs from the digest recorded in the navigation index while preserving the current raw bytes as the returned source..
- Comparison evidence: `COMPARISON_UNKNOWN` · A same-corpus local task compared this implementation's reopened source lines with ripgrep on the same selected source. It shows output overlap and the additional index/digest/budget receipt, not end-to-end search superiority, reliability, or cost. The current registered cluster-ID and exact-asset-ID tools have different input contracts. - Deprecation/retirement: `COMPARISON_UNKNOWN` · Optional default-off candidate; the synthetic AB-02 result and local tool tests do not establish replacement value or justify retirement of another tool.
- Migration/rollback: No Current operation is migrated or changed; existing cluster-ID and exact-record-ID tool contracts remain available. / Remove this candidate entry and wrapper from the isolated toolbox branch, regenerate the identity-card projections, and preserve the implementation/test/evidence commit history..

### `maintenance.attribute_actions_validation_evidence` · Actions 验证计划/工件归因器 / Actions validation plan/artifact attribution

- State: `EXPERIMENTAL_AVAILABLE` / `EXPERIMENTAL` · version `0.1.0` · license `BUSL-1.1` · maintainer `Ignition repository maintainers; this field does not grant Owner or scientific authority.` · source blob `4a37bd62415975999c31f07d83cffeb612b35533`.
- Can do: Align one caller-supplied component validation plan and its selected targets with a GitHub Actions run/jobs snapshot, artifact file list, and optional target receipt.

- Not for: Fetching or authenticating GitHub runs, artifacts, plans, or receipts.; Certifying component validation, selected-target success, required-check satisfaction, test equivalence, or official Foundation status.; Estimating billable minutes, cash cost, waste, runtime savings, or analyst-time savings.; Granting CI sufficiency, merge/release authorization, or scientific validity.
- Use only if: The caller supplies one bounded UTF-8 JSON input with a canonical plan digest, Actions run/jobs snapshot, artifact ID/digest and extracted file list.; Run ID, attempt, and head SHA bind to both the plan and artifact inventory; any supplied pull-request base SHA matches the plan.; The caller treats artifact completeness and optional receipt contents as unauthenticated input assertions.
- Do not use when: The task requires live GitHub acquisition, authenticated source provenance, or a CI policy decision.; The requested answer is billable time, cash cost, avoidable work, required-check satisfaction, merge permission, or release permission.; The caller cannot process the supplied CI and artifact metadata within the current task authority.
- Inputs → outputs: UTF-8 JSON stdin containing one component validation plan, one workflow run/jobs snapshot, one artifact inventory with extracted basenames and completeness flag, and an optional component validation receipt. → Deterministic JSON receipt with plan/run/artifact identity bindings, selected-target IDs, receipt presence or binding status, observed job intervals, source/tool digests, and explicit non-claims.

- Side effects: Reads one bounded stdin JSON envelope and the two local tool sources for digests; emits one JSON receipt to stdout or a validation error to stderr; creates or modifies no files and makes no network request..
- Dependencies: ignition/tools/toolbox_actions_step_timeline.py; runtime dependencies: Python >=3.9 standard library.
- Cost: `UNKNOWN` · No representative analyst-time, latency, CPU, memory, hosted-runner or cash-cost comparison has been measured; the tool is offline.
- Alternatives: `maintenance.summarize_actions_step_timeline` (COMPLEMENTARY_NOT_EQUIVALENT): The existing analyzer validates and summarizes one supplied run/jobs snapshot but has no component plan, selected-target receipt or artifact file-list input. Paired same-run evidence shows the new tool adds that attribution scope while preserving the existing timeline output.; `maintenance.compare_actions_repeated_step_sequences` (COMPLEMENTARY_NOT_EQUIVALENT): The existing analyzer requires two to twenty same-context runs and groups repeated steps; this tool handles one plan-bound run and its artifact inventory. Their useful input scopes differ..
- Replacement candidates: none established.
- Unique remaining use cases: Attribute a single supplied Actions run to a digest-bound validation plan, selected target list, artifact file list, and optional receipt while preserving job conclusions and observed intervals..
- Comparison evidence: `SPECIALIZED_NO_REPLACEMENT_ESTABLISHED` · Paired offline functional comparison on cancelled #264 run 40064357026: both tools produce the same job/step interval summary, while the new tool additionally binds the FULL plan digest, 11 selected target IDs, exact run/head/base, artifact digest/file list, and receipt state. A follow-up same-task route trial measured three local CLI samples: timeline-only median wall/CPU 30.032/27.233 ms and 13,531 input/16,521 output bytes; attribution-only 28.975/27.024 ms and 122,646 input/3,357 output bytes; adding both 54.409/51.481 ms and 136,177 input/19,878 output bytes. These are single-machine, task-local observations, not general performance or cash-cost evidence. The multi-run analyzer requires 2-20 runs and is not applicable to this one-run input. A controlled timeline-plus-attribution counterfactual duplicates the required interval facts and took longer in three local samples; detailed step timing remains a distinct timeline-tool use case.; The repeated-step analyzer requires multiple runs, so its distinct multi-run use case remains uncovered. Keep the existing timeline tool for interval-only and detailed-step tasks, the batch analyzer for multi-run history, and the new tool for plan/artifact/receipt attribution; no existing tool is fully replaced..
- Deprecation/retirement: `SPECIALIZED` · Same-task input and output comparison shows the new plan/artifact/receipt alignment is absent from the single-run timeline contract; the multi-run analyzer has a different 2-20 run input scope. No existing tool is fully replaced.

- Route default: `false` · conditions: Remain excluded unless this exact candidate ID is explicitly allowlisted for a one-run component-validation plan/artifact attribution task.; A route proposal does not invoke the tool, authenticate its inputs, verify official CI evidence, or grant merge/release authority..

### `maintenance.compare_actions_repeated_step_sequences` · GitHub Actions 重复步骤序列分析器 / GitHub Actions repeated step sequence analyzer

- State: `EXPERIMENTAL_AVAILABLE` / `EXPERIMENTAL` · version `0.1.0; reuses toolbox_actions_step_timeline summarize_snapshot contract` · license `BUSL-1.1` · maintainer `Ignition repository maintainers; this field grants no CI, Owner, or release authority.` · source blob `7eaaa9f5f8161ae0f590c059875567fb42c1c421`.
- Can do: Compare repeated job/step names and observed intervals across supplied runs of one workflow, event, pull request, and base commit; keep changed head revisions and source anomalies visible.
- Good for: Finding repeated job/step labels across two to twenty caller-supplied workflow runs for the same workflow, event, pull request, and base SHA.; Reviewing each run and exact head SHA beside repeated step outcomes and observed intervals, including failed and successful runs.; Preserving source timestamp anomalies and partial coverage rather than silently converting them to zero duration.
- Not for: Deciding repeated work was avoidable, wasted, caused by a workflow defect, or safe to remove.; Calculating billable runner minutes, cash cost, user wait, globally elapsed time, or causal value of CI.; Assessing required-check satisfaction, test equivalence, branch protection, merge/release permission, or Foundation validity.; Fetching or authenticating GitHub data; the tool only processes caller-supplied JSON snapshots.; Treating an equal-timestamp skipped step in a cancelled run as a successful execution or as proof that the check ran.
- Use only if: The caller supplies two to twenty complete enough workflow-run/jobs snapshots with one shared workflow ID/path, event, PR number, and base SHA.; Each snapshot can be bound to its run ID, attempt, head SHA, and matching job IDs/attempts/head SHA.; The caller accepts that head SHAs may differ, timestamps may be partial, and input provenance is unauthenticated by this tool.
- Do not use when: Snapshots mix workflows, events, pull requests, or base commits; the analyzer fails closed on mixed contexts.; The task requires a causal waste estimate, exact GitHub billing, a CI policy decision, or an authoritative source signature.; The caller cannot retain or process the supplied workflow metadata within the current task authority.
- Inputs → outputs: UTF-8 JSON stdin object containing 2–20 workflow_run/jobs_response snapshots for one workflow/event/PR/base context → Per-run source-bound-to-input timeline projections retaining each head SHA and run conclusion, Repeated step groups with per-run identifiers, outcomes, timestamps, and observed interval seconds, Explicit timestamp-anomaly list, input/tool digests, and non-claim markers
- Permissions: `READ_ONLY_RUN` / `READ_ONLY_CALLER_SUPPLIED_ACTIONS_SNAPSHOT_BATCH` · explicit Owner authorization required `false` · reads `ignition/tools/toolbox_actions_repeated_step_analysis.py, ignition/tools/toolbox_actions_step_timeline.py` · writes `none` · network `false` hosts `none` · secrets `false` · billing `false` · external actions `false.
- Side effects: Reads bounded UTF-8 JSON from stdin and the analyzer/helper source files for their digests; emits one JSON receipt to stdout or a validation error to stderr; makes no network request and creates or modifies no files., For a completed skipped job with reversed timestamps only, preserves the original times in timestamp_anomalies and nulls both interval endpoints in the derived analysis; all other reversed intervals remain invalid..
- Dependencies: ignition/tools/toolbox_actions_step_timeline.py; runtime dependencies: Python >=3.9 standard library.
- Cost: `UNKNOWN` · No representative CPU, memory, latency, GitHub hosted-runner, or cash-cost profile has been measured; the analyzer itself is offline.
- Alternatives: `maintenance.summarize_actions_step_timeline` (COMPLEMENTARY_NOT_EQUIVALENT): The existing tool analyzes one supplied run. This analyzer reuses its per-run contract and adds cross-run repeated-step grouping for a single PR/base history; neither proves avoidable work..
- Replacement candidates: none established.
- Unique remaining use cases: Compare repeated named steps and their per-head outcomes across two to twenty snapshots from one PR/base context in one call, preserving changed heads and partial-time anomalies..
- Comparison evidence: `SPECIALIZED_NO_REPLACEMENT_ESTABLISHED` · Paired offline analysis of four same-workflow/event/PR/base #264 run snapshots and an isolated single-run smoke. The batch output has 70 repeated-step groups and preserves two timestamp anomalies; no latency, resource, analyst-effort, or causal-waste comparison was performed. Comparators: maintenance.summarize_actions_step_timeline; implementation refs: none recorded; refs: ignition/tools/toolbox_actions_repeated_step_analysis.py, ignition/tools/toolbox_actions_step_timeline.py, ignition/tests/test_toolbox_actions_repeated_step_analysis.py, ignition/tests/fixtures/toolbox-actions-repeated-step-analysis/pr264-four-runs-r0.json, ignition/data/operations/toolbox-actions-repeated-step-evidence-r0.json; findings: The single-run tool emits a per-run timeline and cannot produce cross-run groups; the batch analyzer handles two to twenty context-matched runs and emits repeated-step groups. The batch tool adds a distinct capability without covering the single-run tool's isolated-input use case, so both remain active as specialized default-off tools. Repeated steps, including repeated Foundation commands, are not evidence of avoidable work..
- Deprecation/retirement: `SPECIALIZED` · Four-run comparison demonstrates a multi-run grouping use case that the single-run tool does not cover; single-run analysis remains a distinct useful input scope.
- Migration/rollback: Use the existing single-run candidate for exactly one snapshot; opt into this tool only for two to twenty runs sharing workflow, event, PR, and base SHA.; A reported zero interval for a skipped step is not evidence that the step executed. No Current operation, router default, or existing route changes. / Remove the optional candidate, analyzer, test, fixture, and evidence artifact; regenerate identity cards and TOOLBOX.md; retain source API URLs and historical run IDs in the durable report if the analytical result remains useful..
- Invocation example: `python3 -B ignition/tools/toolbox_actions_repeated_step_analysis.py --stdin`
- Fallback: If context, run/job binding, or timestamps are invalid, report the exact rejection and inspect each run separately with the existing single-run tool; do not convert missing intervals to zero or infer wasted work.

### `maintenance.compare_audit_findings` · 审计发现对照器 / Audit Finding Comparator

- State: `EXPERIMENTAL_AVAILABLE` / `EXPERIMENTAL` · version `0.1.0` · license `BUSL-1.1` · maintainer `Ignition repository maintainers; this field does not grant Owner or scientific authority.` · source blob `74a8f94a31eb196d6f815b986306059fa7262673`.
- Can do: Compare two caller-declared audit records for finding coverage, disposition disagreements, and evidence-reference differences when scope and target revision match; it does not adjudicate findings or establish review independence.
- Good for: Comparing caller-supplied primary and follow-up audit records for one identical scope, target, and target revision.; Showing finding IDs present in only one review, caller-declared disposition disagreements, and differences in evidence-reference sets.; Preparing a bounded comparison for human follow-up while preserving the two original records and their separate evidence references.
- Not for: Performing an audit, reading evidence references, authenticating review records or reviewers, or proving reviewer independence.; Adjudicating claim truth, evidence quality, security severity, scientific validity, or whether a finding is correctly confirmed or rejected.; Comparing records for different scopes, targets, or target revisions; authorizing code changes, releases, or external actions.
- Use only if: Both audit records are caller supplied and bind the same exact scope_ref, target_ref, and target_revision.; The caller needs a structural difference report and accepts that reviewer identity and evidence authenticity remain unverified.; Input contains bounded references and hashes rather than secret, hidden-reasoning, or raw sensitive audit text.
- Do not use when: The records refer to different targets or revisions or require content-level evidence verification.; A result would be treated as independent approval, security certification, scientific validation, or permission to act.; The input includes credentials, hidden reasoning, or raw material that should not enter the local CLI process.
- Inputs → outputs: UTF-8 JSON request matching toolbox-audit-finding-comparison-r0.schema.json with two audit records bound to the same scope_ref, target_ref, and target_revision; each finding includes an ID, caller-declared disposition, and bounded source references with caller-declared SHA-256 values. → Caller-declared finding coverage and disposition relations, source-reference lists, target binding, and explicit non-adjudication / non-independence status in JSON.
- Permissions: `READ_ONLY_RUN` / `READ_ONLY_LOCAL_STDIN_AUDIT_RECORD_COMPARISON` · explicit Owner authorization required `false` · reads `none` · writes `none` · network `false` hosts `none` · secrets `false` · billing `false` · external actions `false.
- Side effects: Reads at most 256000 stdin bytes and writes one comparison JSON result to stdout; it does not read evidence files, write files, contact a network, access secrets, bill, or authorize an action..
- Dependencies: none declared; runtime dependencies: Python >=3.9 standard library.
- Cost: `UNKNOWN` · Input is capped at 256000 bytes and 100 findings per record; runtime, memory, and any human follow-up cost have not been calibrated.
- Alternatives: `research.validate_evidence_program_snapshot` (COMPLEMENTARY_NOT_EQUIVALENT): Runs the fixed repository evidence-program validator and returns a source-tree digest; it does not compare two review finding sets.; `maintenance.attribute_actions_validation_evidence` (COMPLEMENTARY_NOT_EQUIVALENT): Maps a validation plan and its targets to one Actions run snapshot; it does not reconcile audit disposition or evidence-reference differences.; `knowledge.navigate_raw_evidence` (COMPLEMENTARY_NOT_EQUIVALENT): Finds bounded raw source lines in the checked-out Knowledge index; it does not compare caller-supplied review records.; `maintenance.compare_context_packet_variants` (COMPLEMENTARY_NOT_EQUIVALENT): Compares same-task ContextPacket excerpt and obligation structures, not audit finding records..
- Replacement candidates: none established.
- Unique remaining use cases: Compare two caller-provided audit records bound to identical scope, target, and revision and list finding and evidence-reference differences without adjudicating either record..
- Comparison evidence: `SPECIALIZED_NO_REPLACEMENT_ESTABLISHED` · R0 is limited to same-target caller-record comparison for finding coverage, declared dispositions, and evidence-reference sets; it does not perform independent verification. Comparators: research.validate_evidence_program_snapshot, maintenance.attribute_actions_validation_evidence, knowledge.navigate_raw_evidence, maintenance.compare_context_packet_variants; implementation refs: none recorded; refs: ignition/data/operations/toolbox-audit-finding-comparator-c1-evidence-r0.json; findings: The existing validator checks one fixed evidence program, the attribution tool binds run data to planned targets, the navigator locates raw repository lines, and the ContextPacket comparator measures a different pair of structural packets.; No compared tool consumes two audit records and reports finding coverage, caller-declared disposition differences, and evidence-reference differences under an exact shared target binding.; This bounded function has no evidence of replacing any compared tool; no existing tool is deprecated or retired..
- Deprecation/retirement: `SPECIALIZED` · The candidate has a narrow record-comparison input/output contract; comparison evidence does not support replacing another tool.
- Migration/rollback: Optional default-off candidate only; no Current operation, report authority, or permission changes.; Callers may supply immutable audit records by piping them to stdin; the tool does not migrate or rewrite those records. / Remove this candidate card, implementation, schemas, tests, generated identity projection, and its route/C1/task evidence from the isolated candidate.; Keep existing audit records and all compared tools unchanged..

- State: `EXPERIMENTAL_AVAILABLE` / `EXPERIMENTAL` · version `0.1.0` · license `BUSL-1.1` · maintainer `Ignition repository maintainers; this field does not grant Owner or scientific authority.` · source blob `1487adecbd8be43c2ecbbdec9dd43047a097cbe4`.

### `maintenance.preflight_producer_consumer_semantics` · 生产者—消费者语义预检器 / Producer-Consumer Semantic Preflight

- State: `EXPERIMENTAL_AVAILABLE` / `EXPERIMENTAL` · version `0.1.0` · license `BUSL-1.1` · maintainer `Ignition repository maintainers; this field does not grant Owner or scientific authority.` · source blob `4ecc9f39dd0c6bcfdc6844685ccac61f7024e4da`.
- Can do: Statically inspect one bounded caller-supplied Python consumer function for direct constant re.search checks and compare them with caller-supplied producer text; never import or execute the consumer.
- Good for: Checking whether a bounded producer text contains the direct constant regex records required by one supported consumer function.; Distinguishing missing, case-variant, malformed or reordered, duplicate, and contradictory required records without returning raw captured field values.; Producing a local diagnostic before a separately authorized runtime acceptance check when the consumer uses the narrowly supported direct re.search subset.
- Not for: Authenticating the caller, source repository, Git revision, expected digest, or producer provenance.; Proving the complete consumer accepts the producer, that producer and consumer are semantically equivalent, or that any task or scientific claim is correct.; Evaluating dynamic regex construction, arbitrary transformations, nested parser logic, arbitrary Python semantics, or runtime side effects.; Editing a producer, changing a frozen handoff, importing or executing an old consumer, or restarting an experiment.
- Use only if: The caller provides one UTF-8 JSON input object within the 262144-byte cap, with consumer source up to 131072 UTF-8 bytes and producer text up to 65536 UTF-8 bytes.; The caller-supplied expected SHA-256 values bind the exact strings supplied in the same input; they are digests, not authenticated provenance.; The selected function uses only direct constant re.search(pattern, text_name) calls over a direct string argument or a single UTF-8 decode, with all result variables jointly guarded by a simple fail-closed raise.
- Do not use when: Actual runtime acceptance, full parser behavior, arbitrary control flow, or semantic interpretation must be tested.; Patterns are dynamic, transformed, nested, unsupported by the bounded regex subset, or not jointly fail-closed.; The decision requires authenticated provenance, permission enforcement, scientific review, external truth, or publication approval.
- Inputs → outputs: One UTF-8 JSON object containing a Python consumer source excerpt, function name, expected SHA-256, producer text, and its expected SHA-256; stdin is capped at 262144 bytes. → One bounded deterministic JSON result with source digest bindings, per-pattern and field match statuses/counts/digests, redacted captures, and all non-execution/non-authentication claim flags false.
- Permissions: `READ_ONLY_RUN` / `READ_ONLY_BOUNDED_STDIN_AND_LOCAL_INPUT_SCHEMA` · explicit Owner authorization required `false` · reads `ignition/schemas/operations/toolbox-producer-consumer-semantic-input-r0.schema.json` · writes `none` · network `false` hosts `none` · secrets `false` · billing `false` · external actions `false.
- Side effects: Reads bounded JSON from stdin and the local input schema; writes one bounded JSON result to stdout only..
